MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Wesley D. Bay, P.E., Deputy City Engineer 
 
DATE: 
February 14, 2022 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Service Agreement for Professional Land Surveying Services. 
 
 
Please find the attached Professional Service Agreement between the City and Steil Surveying 
Services, LLC. for surveying services for the City of Cheyenne. 
  
Engineering Staff evaluates fee proposals for engineering services based on time and material 
estimates for the specific project.  Those costs are readily tracked and verified during 
design/review operations and the City only pays for the actual time expended. We believe this 
agreement will provide the required services to complete the surveying related tasks necessary for 
the review of City and developer projects. 
 
City funding for these services will come from 2019 – 2022 1% sales tax. The total professional 
services will be a cost not-to-exceed $25,000.00. We respectfully request your approval of this 
professional services agreement. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

1 
 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
Professional Land Surveying Services 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _______ day of ________________, 2022, by 
and between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Steil Surveying Services, LLC., 1102 West 19th Street, Cheyenne, WY 82001 
(“Consultant”). 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and 
between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by reference 
and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by January 30, 2023, as stated 
in Exhibit A. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant 
will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of Twenty-
Five Thousand Dollars ($25,000.00). 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the Consultant, 
the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not meeting 
such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 2:45 pm, Feb 08, 2022

2 
 
 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of Cheyenne 
has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to alcohol and 
drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers and other consultants under 
contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are required to 
comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy for drug 
and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites occupied by the Consultant while 
performing the duties and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the responsibility of the 
Consultant to familiarize themselves with the requirements of this policy and to inform all 
their employees and sub-consultants of the requirements and ensure their compliance. If 
the Consultant, their employees or sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the 
Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result 
the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any person, 
destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or sub-
consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges that it 
may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 per 
occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 

3 
 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided 
pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, the 
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a 
new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be 
in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services under 
this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., 
b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance 
written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall provide the 
City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) 
which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than five (5) years 
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this 
Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the above-
stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All policies 
required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  It shall 
be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify in writing the city risk 
manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has 
resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance 
coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach 
of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  

4 
 
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not 
affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a written 
statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, and 
work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of any 
payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that 
may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this 
Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to 
act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of 
any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts 
and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
 

5 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from the date of execution to January 30, 2023.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon 
receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Consultant 
for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. 
Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the 
City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of default 
of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to 
commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its 
own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming.

immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES

performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 

6 
 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts

examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement;

20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT

pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne and

21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair

Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age

FORCE MAJEURE.

acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental regulation or

The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without liability, damages, fees, or

PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION

ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT

MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (MOU), by and between the Carbon Asset Network 
(CAN), located at 113 East 20th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and the City of Cheyenne 
(City), a municipal corporation existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming, located at 2101 
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, is entered into and becomes effective on the date of 
the last signature affixed hereto. 
 
WHEREAS, CAN was formed in 2020 as a resource for land managers through the help 
of nonprofits that support soil health, in order to empower the land managers to be a better 
steward of their land.   
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this MOU is to set forth the terms and conditions agreed 
to by the parties regarding the partnership to empower the managers to be a better steward of 
their land.   
 
2. 
Term.  This MOU is effective until terminated by written agreement between the 
parties. 
 
3. 
Process.  CAN recruits land managers into CAN.  
a. 
CAN and land manager will enter into a separate Memorandum of 
Understanding which will require that the land manager will receive funds in exchange 
for working their acreage in a more regenerative and sustainable manner for twelve (12) 
months.   
b. 
CAN’s agronomist will conduct extensive soil testing of the acreage and 
produce a report creating benchmarks for soil organic carbon and other soil elements.  
This sampling is “boots on the ground” sampling and not based on satellite imagery or 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:36 am, Feb 23, 2022

 
Page 2 of 6 
estimations, which are less granular and can only estimate 6 inches below the surface.  
The soil is, instead, sampled at 0 inches to 6 inches and 6 inches to 24 inches for a 
comprehensive test as native plant roots extend to at least 24 inches into the soil profile. 
c. 
The agronomist will send soil samples to a certified lab, upon which their 
analysis will create a report establishing the carbon baseline.  The results of the report 
will then be uploaded onto blockchain.   
d. 
The land manager and CAN’s agronomist will review the results and 
create together a regenerative plan that is customized based on soil content and land 
manager’s goals.  
e. 
The land manager will then execute this plan for twelve (12) months. 
f. 
Sampling will occur again at the conclusion of the first twelve (12) months 
in the same locations as the first samples that were recorded by GPS.  The results will 
then be posted to blockchain and the status of the soil organic carbon is then noted.  
g. 
If the land manager chooses to continue, they meet with agronomist to 
amend the plan and then execute for another twelve (12) months, repeating for as long as 
both parties wish to continue.  
h. 
CAN is responsible for all soil sampling, agronomy consultancy, lab 
analyses, and blockchain operations. 
4. 
Expectations of City of Cheyenne. 
The City will verify that CAN is executing the process outlined above. 
5.  
Expectations of CAN  
CAN will provide the City with the necessary documents to allow the City to verify the 
process outlined in paragraph 4 above.  For serving as verifier of protocol, CAN will pay the 

 
Page 3 of 6 
City two percent (2%) of the granted donations received from the nonprofit.  CAN will complete 
the requirements of the process outlined in paragraph 3 above.  
 
6. 
General Provisions. 
 
 
a. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to 
this MOU which are mutually agreed upon by the parties to this MOU shall be 
incorporated by written instrument, executed and signed by all parties to this MOU. 
b. 
Assignment.  No party may assign or transfer the responsibilities or 
agreements made herein without the prior written consent of the non-assigning parties, 
which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
c. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this 
MOU shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this MOU and the parties, and the venue shall be in 
the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
d. 
Compliance with Laws.  The parties shall keep informed of and comply 
with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations in the performance of this 
MOU. 
  
e. 
Indemnification.  Each party to this MOU assumes the risk of any liability 
arising from its own conduct.  Each party agrees that it will be responsible for any loss, 
claim, damages, or demands resulting from its negligence and the negligence of its 
employees and agents.  Neither party agrees to insure, defend, or indemnify the other. 
f. 
Nondiscrimination.  The parties shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 
1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., the 
Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the 

 
Page 4 of 6 
Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations 
thereto, and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, 
creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy, or qualifying disability 
in connection with the performance under this MOU. 
g. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City does not waive governmental 
immunity by entering into this MOU and specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all 
other applicable laws.  Designation of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and 
similar provisions should not be construed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
h. 
Third-Party Beneficiary Rights.  The parties do not intend to create in any 
other individual or entity, the status of third-party beneficiary and this MOU shall not be 
construed so as to create such status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this 
MOU shall operate only between the parties to this MOU and shall inure solely to the 
benefit of the parties to this MOU. The provisions of this MOU are intended only to 
assist the parties in determining and performing their obligations under this MOU. The 
parties to this MOU intend and expressly agree that only party signatory to this MOU 
shall have any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this MOU, to seek any remedy 
arising out of a party’s performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this 
MOU, or to bring an action for the breach of this MOU. 
i. 
Force Majeure.  The performance of this MOU by the parties shall be 
subject to force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural 
disaster, war or threat of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized 
strikes, governmental regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by 

 
Page 5 of 6 
the World Health Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government 
authority or health agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-
19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other 
similar occurrence beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors, 
circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or 
unreasonably delay the performance required by this MOU.  This MOU may be cancelled 
by any party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or 
amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written 
notice to the other parties. 
j. 
Severability.  If any provision of this MOU is found to be invalid or 
unenforceable for any reason, the remaining provisions will continue to be valid and 
enforceable.  If a court finds that any provision of this MOU is invalid or unenforceable, 
but that by limiting such provision it would become valid and enforceable, then such 
provision will be deemed to be written, construed, and enforced as so limited. 
k. 
Notice.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU 
shall be in writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
l. 
Termination.  Any party may terminate its participation in this MOU by 
providing thirty (30) days written notice to other party. 
m. 
Prior Approval.  This MOU shall not be binding upon the parties, no 
services shall be performed under the terms of this MOU, and no payments shall be made 
until this MOU has been reduced to writing and approved by all necessary authorities. 
n. 
Entirety of Contract.  This MOU represents the entire and integrated 
agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and 

 
Page 6 of 6 
agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
7. 
Signatures.  In witness thereof, the parties to this MOU, through their duly 
authorized representatives, have executed this MOU on the days and dates set out below and 
certify that they have read, understood, and knowingly and voluntarily agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this MOU.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-28_TotalExtraction_71253969.pdf
CONTRACT NO. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made by and between the THE BOARD OF PUBLIC UTILITIES OF 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, hereinafter referred to as “BOPU,” and POLO RANCH 
COMPANY, hereinafter referred to as “Polo Ranch.” 
 
RECITALS 
 
WHEREAS, Polo Ranch and the City of Cheyenne are parties to a Drilling and Water 
Use Agreement entered into in 1955; and 
 
WHEREAS, over the past several years there have been numerous disputes between the 
parties which have led to litigation and multiple court cases; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Wyoming courts have upheld the validity of the 1955 agreement and the 
City’s interpretation of its meaning; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Polo Ranch now seeks to develop twelve (12) lots, for a total of not 
more than twelve (12) wells, in the Quarter Circle Five subdivision; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has an interest in additional development for the City 
and its residents; and 
 
WHEREAS, the parties wish to enter into this Agreement; 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, for good and valuable consideration, the receipt of which is hereby 
acknowledged by each party, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. BOPU/City agrees to allow Polo Ranch to develop the twelve (12) lots with (12) twelve 
corresponding wells in the Quarter Circle Five subdivision. See attached Exhibit. 
2. This change in use does not affect the validity or enforceability of the City’s water rights 
under the 1955 Agreement and does not increase the City’s obligations under the 1955 
Agreement. 
3. This change in use applies to Polo Ranch’s entire allocation of water per the formula 
under the 1955 Agreement. 
4. BOPU/City shall have no obligation to provide water to or for the Polo Ranch, for any 
use, including water for the lots in Quarter Circle Five, in excess of that determined by 
the formula in the 1955 Agreement. 
5. The deduction from the Polo Ranch’s annual water entitlement for the Quarter Circle 
Five subdivision lots shall remain .7 acre feet per lot. 
6. The Polo Ranch agrees to add deed restrictions to each lot sold to prevent further splitting 
of the twelve (12) lots. 
7. The Polo Ranch agrees to restrict the size of the lawns of the twelve (12) lots within 
Quarter Circle Five subdivision to a maximum of 6,500 square feet. 
By Stefanie Boster at 2:54 pm, Feb 18, 2022

8. Except for the consent of the City as set forth in paragraph 1 above, this Agreement shall 
not affect the rights and duties of the parties under the 1955 Agreement. This Agreement 
shall not be deemed to constitute an amendment of the 1955 Agreement. 
9. In the event any additional documents are necessary to carry out the intent of this 
Agreement, the parties hereto agree to execute and/or obtain such documents promptly 
upon the request of the other party. 
 
DATED this  
day of  
,2022. 
 
 
BOARD OF PUBLIC UTILITIES OF 
POLO RANCH COMPANY 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
 
 
By:  
 
By:  
 
Mary Guthrie, President 
President 
 
 
Attest:  
 
Attest:  
 
Secretary 
Secretary

CITY OF CHEYENNE

FIRST AMENDMENT TO CITY CONTRACT 7118 
 THE MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING BETWEEN

1. 
Parties.  This First Amendment to the Memorandum of Understanding (the "Amendment")

Wyoming, 82001. 
 
2. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this Amendment is to extend the time for performance of the 
party’s respective obligations described in the Memorandum of Understanding due to mutual 
delays relating to Covid 19. 
 
3. 
Recitals. 
 
 
a. 
Saddle Ridge and the City entered into a Memorandum of Understanding, also 
known as City Contract number 7118, with an effective date of January 24, 2020 (the “MOU”).

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-28_TotalExtraction_30044382.pdf
1 
 
Modification #1 to Contract #7405 
 
CONTINGENCY AND DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT 
 
Microsoft Datacenter:  2021 Expansion 
 
 
This Agreement is entered into by and between the City of Cheyenne, a municipal 
corporation organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming (“City”), 2101 O’Neil Avenue, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and Microsoft (Company), a Washington corporation (“Company”), 
This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the last signature affixed hereto. 
 
WHEREAS, the purpose of the Business Ready Community Program (“BRC”) is to 
promote economic development at the city, town, and county levels in order to create additional 
economic health and a stronger state economy; and 
 
WHEREAS, the BRC program allows for grants to defray the costs of communication and 
electrical utilities to encourage the development, recruitment or expansion of data generation, and 
information technology storage operations; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City is an eligible Applicant to the BRC; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City will comply and adhere to the Wyoming Preference Act (Wyo. Stat. 
§§ 16-6-201 through 16-6-206) and all other Wyoming Statutes that apply; and 
WHEREAS, the Company will contribute economically to the community by creating jobs, 
investing in development of facilities, and broadening the City’s economic and tax bases. 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the promises and agreements contained herein, 
the parties agree as follows: 
 
Article I. 
Project Description 
 
 
The City shall apply for and obtain for the ultimate benefit of the Company, a BRC grant 
for the purpose of reducing operating costs at the Company’s data center facility.  It is anticipated 
by the parties that these reduced costs will provide public benefits by allowing the Company to 
generate revenues, increase employment, make investments, and otherwise expand the beneficial 
economic impact of its operations in the City of Cheyenne and the State of Wyoming. 
 
Article II. 
City’s Responsibilities 
 
 
 The City shall: 
 
A. 
Prepare and submit a BRC grant application for a Grant (“Grant”) in the amount of 
Two Million Two Hundred Fifty Thousand Dollars ($2,250,000.00). 
 
B. 
Accept the Grant award upon approval by the Wyoming State Loan and Investment 
board and undertake all project administration requirements of the Grant. 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:39 am, Feb 25, 2022

2 
 
 
C. 
Contingent upon award of the Grant and upon receipt of invoices substantiating 
communication and electrical utility expenses incurred by the Company including, 
without limitation, any such costs incurred in the commissioning of its facility, and 
upon receipt by the City or its agent from the Company of evidence towards 
meeting public benefit requirements set forth below, the City shall seek 
reimbursement of 100% of these costs from the Grant as provided under guidance 
from the Wyoming Business Council.  The Company shall provide City with 
detailed invoices, which shall be submitted to the City’s Grants Manager.  Payment 
shall be made in accordance with Wyo. Stat. § 16-6-602 (as amended). 
 
Article III. 
Company’s Responsibilities  
 
The Company shall, contingent upon (i) the award of the Grant on terms acceptable to the 
Company by not later than June 30, 2022, and (ii) the Company’s obtaining all necessary permits 
(collectively, the “Conditions”): 
 
A. 
Provide in consideration for the Grant, the following public benefits: 
1.  
Expand its existing data center operations in the City of Cheyenne by 
investing approximately Fifty Million Dollars ($50,000,000.00) in on-site 
site development and/or construction. 
2.  
Pay or invest no less than Fifty-One Million Four Hundred Six Thousand 
Two Hundred Fifty Dollars ($51,406,250.00) of combined wages and 
capital expenditures within five (5) years of first receiving Grant funds. No 
less than One Million Four Hundred Six Thousand and Two Hundred Fifty 
Dollars ($1,406,250.00) of this amount shall be wages. The wages counted 
towards these amounts must be paid to positions with wage rates that exceed 
$24.14 per hour (150% of most recently announced average weekly wage 
for Laramie County per Wyoming Department of Employment as of July 1, 
2021). 
 
3.  
Microsoft will make good-faith efforts to work with the City, the political 
subdivision, and Laramie County on community initiatives once the data 
center is operational.   
 
 
B. 
Company will provide to the City an annual certification of payroll and investment 
as evidence towards meeting the public benefit requirements set forth above for a 
period of five (5) years. Furthermore, the Company understands that additional 
documentation or evidence of payrolls and investment or other public benefit may 
be required by the Wyoming Business Council and the Company agrees to provide 
such additional documentation or evidence as may be reasonably required to 
substantiate the amount of wages and capital expenditures paid or invested by the 
Company. 
 
 
C. 
Promptly repay with all Grant funds paid to it during the elapsed term of the Grant 
by the City plus interest at 2% per annum, in the event that it ceases operating its 

3 
 
data center facility in the City of Cheyenne within five (5) years of first receiving 
Grant funds from the City.  Temporary cessations of operations arising out of 
casualty 
damage, 
or 
repairs, 
maintenance, 
server 
migrations, 
or 
renovations/upgrades shall not be considered ceasing operations for purposes of 
this Subsection C.  Repayment of such Grant funds including the rate of return shall 
be the exclusive remedy of the City for any failure by the Company to perform its 
obligations under this Article III. 
 
 
D. 
Provide information, assistance, and consultation to the City in the preparation and 
submission of the BRC application. 
 
E. 
Maintain any and all records, documents, and data pertaining to job creation, wages 
paid, capital investment, and other consideration provided in exchange for the Grant 
funds and, subject to the Company’s reasonable confidentiality requirements, 
provide these records to the City upon request by the City.  Company’s records are 
deemed confidential business information and trade secret and will not be subject 
to Freedom of Information Act requests. 
F. 
Comply with all other requirements of the Grant, to the extent approved by the 
Company at the time of Grant award. 
 
Article IV. 
Parties Common Agreements 
 
A. 
The parties acknowledge and agree that the period of payments from the Grant is 
three (3) years from the date of the Grant agreement between the Wyoming 
Business Council and the City.  Both parties acknowledge and agree that the 
performance period of the Company to provide public benefits is five (5) years from 
the date of the first payment of Grant funds to the Company.  
 
B. 
The parties agree that this Agreement is contingent upon receipt of Grant funds by 
the City and is intended to meet the first level of convincing evidence of 
development and public benefit as required by the BRC program. 
 
C. 
The parties agree that in the event that the City does not receive an award of the 
Grant from the Wyoming Business Council by June 30, 2022, that no party has any 
obligation under this Agreement and that this Agreement is void unless extended 
in writing by the parties. 
Article V. 
Breach and Termination 
Subject to the provisions of Article III, Subsection C, in the event that any party breaches 
this Agreement the other parties shall be entitled to an injunction or other appropriate remedy in 
order to compel the breaching party to abide by its obligations set forth herein. 
 
 
 
 

4 
 
Article VI. 
Miscellaneous 
A. 
This Agreement consisting of six (6) pages represents the entire and integrated 
agreement and understanding between the parties and supersedes all prior 
negotiations, statements, representations, and agreements, whether written or oral. 
B. 
Neither this Agreement, nor any rights or obligations hereunder, shall be assigned 
or delegated by a party without the prior written consent of the other party. 
C. 
This Agreement shall be modified only by a written agreement, duly executed by 
all parties hereto and with the approval of the Wyoming Business Council’s Chief 
Executive Officer. 
D. 
The parties understand and agree this Agreement shall be governed by and 
interpreted pursuant to the laws of the State of Wyoming without regard to its 
conflicts of laws principles. If any dispute arises between the parties from or 
concerning this Agreement or the subject matter hereof, any suit or proceeding at 
law or in equity shall be brought in the District Court of the State of Wyoming, 
First Judicial District, sitting at Cheyenne, Wyoming. The foregoing provisions of 
this paragraph are agreed by the parties to be a material inducement to the Company 
and the City in executing this Agreement.  
E. 
The City does not waive its Governmental/Sovereign Immunity, as provided by any 
applicable law including Wyo. Stat. §§ 1-39-101, et seq., (hereinafter referred to as 
the “Act”) by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for the 
parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of this 
Agreement.  Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and similar 
provisions should not be construed as a waiver of sovereign immunity.  Any actions 
or claims against the City under this Agreement, to the extent that the same are 
permissible under the terms of this Section and/or applicable law, must be brought 
in accordance with the procedural requirements of the Act.  Further, the City fully 
retains all immunities and defenses provided by law with regard to any action, 
whether in tort, contract or any other theory of law, based on this Agreement. 
F. 
Nothing in this Agreement is intended by the parties or shall be construed to create 
an employer/employee or principal/agent relationship between the parties.  The 
parties do not intend to create in any other individual or entity the status of third-
party beneficiary, and this Agreement is not to be construed so as to create such 
status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this Agreement shall operate 
only between the parties to this Agreement and shall inure solely to the benefit of 
the parties to this Agreement. 
G. 
The Company shall release, indemnify, and hold harmless the City and its officers, 
agents, employees, successors and assigns from any cause of action, or claims or 
demands arising out of the Company’s performance of its obligations under to the 
City under Article III, Sections A.2 and A.3, and Article III, Sections B through E, 
of this Agreement. 

5 
 
H. 
The Company shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., and the Americans with 
Disabilities Act (ADA), 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq. The Company shall assure that 
no person is discriminated against based on the grounds of age, sex, race, religion, 
national origin, or disability in connection with the performance of this Agreement.  
I. 
The parties agree to comply with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and 
regulations in the performance of this Agreement.  
J. 
All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this Agreement shall be in 
writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
K. 
The term of this Agreement is from the date of the last signature affixed below 
through five (5) years from the receipt by the Company of the first payment of 
Grant funds provided under this Agreement.

6

MEMORANDUM

Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
February 23, 2022   
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-11-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Z 
& Z Asphalt, for the 2022 Crack Seal #1 project 
______________________________________________ 
 
Two (2) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  A project vicinity map 
is also included to show where the Crack Seal will be taking place. 
 
The Two (2) bids were received from: 
 
1. Z & Z Asphalt. Billings, Montana. 
 
2. Superior Asphalt, LLC.  Magna, Utah. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Doug Klahn (1% Construction), and their recommendation is to accept 
the lowest responsive bid from Z & Z Asphalt, for a total bid award amount of $518,477.96

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-28_TotalExtraction_20874327.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 28, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave., and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and 
Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Johnson and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #21 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon 
request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item 
#26(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 14, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for renewal applications for existing City liquor 
licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite 
distillery, special malt beverage and winery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  There 
being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed 
the public hearing and referred the applications to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership and location of retail 
liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Dillon Companies, LLC, a Kansas 
limited liability company d/b/a King Soopers, 3702 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(license currently held by Avanti, Inc., d/b/a Dell Range Liquor, 3806 Dell Range Blvd. #4, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Kevin Georges expressed concern regarding the proposed transfer of the 
license to a major corporation and recent prior retail liquor license transfers that have went to large 
corporations.  Mr. Georges expressed the need of smaller local businesses having the opportunity 
to obtain retail liquor licenses. Matt Kim-Miller, attorney with Holland & Hart, LLP (applicant’s 
representative), provided information on the request noting the transfer involves a long-standing 
package liquor store location which will be expanded, noting he believes the location is appropriate 
for continued package liquor services for the community.  Upon inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones 
explained the license was originally issued several years ago and the request is for a transfer of 
location and ownership and that the additional retail liquor license awarded to the City based on 
the 2020 census has not been awarded yet.  Comment was made regarding the lack of liquor 
licensing options in the state.   Upon further inquiry, Ms. Jones advised it was her understanding 

that King Soopers will expand the existing package liquor location and open the access from the 
King Soopers store into the current liquor store location.  Mr. Kim-Miller confirmed the plan is to 
expand the existing liquor store space into the prior Hallmark location and there will be a doorway 
into King Soopers where point of sale will occur. Upon inquiry, Mr. Kim-Miller advised the 
proposal is to have doors between the grocery and liquor spaces.  There being no further comments 
or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and 
referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent 
to enter into a contract with Comea Shelter to grant a subaward from Coronavirus State and Local 
Fiscal Recovery Funds to be used to increase available emergency housing and temporary shelter 
for Cheyenne’s homeless population.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 13.04 – Public Utilities Generally; 
Chapter 13.08 – Water Service System; Chapter 13.12 – Sewer System; Chapter 13.16 – Storm 
Sewers; and Chapter 13.20 – Wastewater Treatment & Disposal; of Title 13 – Public Services, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. White moved to amend by 
substitute dated February 17, 2022, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Brad Brooks, 
Director of Board of Public Utilities, provided information on the proposed substitute noting the 
language changes in sections 13.04.050 and 13.04.120.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4416) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts 
and Uses, amending the Permissibility of Various Uses.  Brenda Birkle and Dan Dorsch, 
Affordable Housing Task Force, spoke in support of the ordinance noting it will contribute to 
affordable housing in the community.  Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and Development, 
provided information on a request to include a change for “Entertainment, Amusement, 
Recreation-Other” from a Conditional Use to a Permitted use in the CBD zoning district noting 
the change would remove the requirement for these types of businesses in the downtown area to 
go before the Board of Adjustment for approval resulting in being able to go into business sooner.  
Mr. Bloom advised staff has reviewed the change request and believes it is appropriate for the 
downtown CBD zone.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend Attachment “A” to change 
“Entertainment, Amusement, Recreation-Other” from a Conditional Use to a Permitted Use in the 
CBD zoning district, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry related to a scenario where an 
outdoor shooting range business could potentially open a business in the downtown area without 
notification, Mr. Bloom and Mr. Johnson provided information on existing ordinances that would 
prevent that type of business in the downtown area.  Motion to amend by Attachment A carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  
 

ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint.  Mr. Cook moved 
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to amend by substitute 
dated February 24, 2022, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and 
Development, provided information on the substitute noting the acreage was changed from 100 to 
80 for when developments can submit a common landscape plan.  Motion to amend by substitute 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use to AG Agricultural for 
land located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to HI Heavy 
Industrial for land located north of Campstool Road, west of Progress Circle.  Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Cook confirmed the zone change request is to allow for outdoor storage.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 9.08.120, Malicious Harassment 
Based on Race, Color, Religion, Ancestry, Sex, Sexual Orientation, Gender Identity and 
Expression, National Origin, or Disability, of Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and 
Decency, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  
Mayor Collins announced there would be a 3-minute time limit for speakers and that comments 
and discussion should remain cordial. Speaking in opposition of the ordinance were: Robin 
Goodspeed, Eric Crosby, Jay Meyer, Andrew Dimit, Steven Edgar Olson, Nick Dawson, Alicia 
Green, Bob Wilson, Steve Milliad, Matt Freeman, Angelina Freeman, Sandra Cane and Mark   
Moss.  Topics discussed included: existing statutes, ordinances and policies that address bullying, 
harassment and violence; potential of ordinance being harmful towards individuals with opposing 
beliefs; affect the ordinance may have on religious groups; national events relating to similar 
ordinances in other states; confusion and challenges relating to the ordinance; biblical beliefs; how 
reporting offences to the Police Department will be handled; concern that non-supporters of the 
ordinance don’t support the protected groups outlined in the ordinance; belief that Cheyenne is not 
in need of the ordinance; suggestion to approach harassment concerns in other ways and request 
to allow more community input related to the ordinance.  Speaking in support of the ordinance 
were: Amber Leyba and Dale Steenbergen, Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, Troy 
Rumpf, Stuart Campbell and Keil Bergeron.  Topics discussed included: ordinance importance 
relating to economic development; witnessed threats and slurs; importance of ordinance as a 
statement of support for the protected groups and the need for the ordinance.  Additional comments 

were made that some companies have concerns related to locating in Cheyenne and recruitment as 
there are perceptions that issues and biased attitudes exist locally, and their desire is for every staff 
member to be treated with respect.  It was also noted that some military members stationed here 
have experienced harassment by local businesses.  It was acknowledged that laws exist on the 
federal level and opposing views were voiced on enforcement of those laws.  Reporting for 
Committee of the Whole, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Johnson.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by adding “political affiliation” to the appropriate sections 
throughout the ordinance that reference the protected classes, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Dr. 
Aldrich advised the purpose of the amendment is due to past physical attacks and vandalism at 
local political party’s headquarters.  It was noted other states have included political affiliation in 
similar ordinances.  Upon inquiry, Kris Jones, City Clerk, advised it would be appropriate to 
include “political affiliation” to other sections in the ordinance for clarification of referenced 
protected groups and noted that was the intention of the amendment.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes” all members of the governing body.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend Section 9.08.120 
by removing Section A-3.  Motion died due to lack of a second.  Dr. Aldrich moved to amend 
Section 9.08.120 by adding a new Section E – “This Ordinance will be considered null and void 
when applied to citizens in legitimate exercise of rights enumerated under Article 1, Sections 2, 3, 
20, and 36 of the Wyoming Constitution and the First Amendment of the United States 
Constitution.  Motion died due to lack of a second.   References were made to previous Supreme 
Court cases involving constitutional protected class cases, some of which involved religious 
affiliations and beliefs.  Appreciation was expressed to everyone who has put effort into the 
ordinance, and it was further noted that everyone in the community should be protected.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”.  
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.20, 
Cemeteries, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne 
pertaining to Cemetery fees.  Mr. White moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
repealing UDC 5.4.7, UU – Urban Use Overlay District, and creating UDC 5.6.9, UU – Urban Use 
Overlay District – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from P Public District to CB Community Business 
for land located southwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Cleveland Avenue and U.S. 30 – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Adopting the revised City of Cheyenne, Wyoming Investment Policy 
Statement.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6210) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and file the Wyoming 
Division of Victim Services Funding Application State Fiscal Year 2023 and 2024, July 1, 2022 
to June 30, 2024.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6211)  
 

 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Sweetgrass, 9th Filing, a Replat of all of Lot 1, Block 4, Sweetgrass, 1st Filing situated in the N1/2 
of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located south of East Murray Drive west and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive).  
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning, agent for the owner, advised a replat was previously planned 
for a multi-family parcel in the Sweetgrass PUD.  He provided details of the Sweetgrass 
development history noting the family who owned the property supported multi-family 
development and explained that in general the Sweetgrass development has been decades in the 
planning.  Mr. Palma advised the master plan included a 7.68 acre plat for 168 units which is now 
for 116 units which is within the provisions of PlanCheyenne for urban residential stipulations and 
noted the exception is the PUD is unique.  He provided additional information on proposed open 
space, land reserved for public schools and public parks.  Comments and concerns were raised 
regarding proposed density of the project and related anticipated reduction of street infrastructure.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. Palma reported it is anticipated there will be many additional filings relating to 
the continued development of Sweetgrass which will take years to complete.  Following concerns 
and comments regarding setting a precedence on high density and size requirements with the plat, 
Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and Development, advised that every plat filed will be 
considered on its own.  Comment was made that the proposed plat is fully compliant with the PUD 
as it is written today.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Concern was expressed 
relating to 400 people residing in the vicinity if the final plat is approved, possible issues with 
homeowner’s associations relating to maintenance and the challenge to provide adequate parking.  
Motion failed due to a tie vote.  Voting “yes” - Mayor Collins, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne 
and Mr. Roybal. Voting “no” - Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Segrave and Mr. 
White.  
 
 
RESOLUTION – Adopting a fee schedule for Planning and Development projects. Brad 
Emmons, AVI, expressed appreciation to Planning & Development Director Charles Bloom for 
his efforts on the resolution.  Mr. Emmons voiced concern regarding the proposed revised fee 
schedule and the proposed requirement to obtain a property appraisal for right-of-way vacations, 
as currently appraisals are taking approximately 8 weeks to complete.  Mr. White moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Mr. White moved to amend Attachment “A” by substitute dated 
February 22, 2022, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and 
Development, provided details for the proposed substitute which includes a change to the street 
vacation fee and noted it is up to the governing body if they choose to require the proposed 
appraisal requirement and fee.  Motion to amend Attachment “A” carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom provided information on the $50.00 
permit fee explaining it is due to the amount of Planning and Development staff time required to 
perform reviews of numerous permits.  He noted the proposed fee would generate approximately 
$23,000.00 annually.  John Edwards, Saddle Ridge Subdivision, expressed concern on fee 
increases that will have an effect on affordable housing.  Mr. Bloom provided information on staff 
review time on the different types of plats submitted and requested consideration to require a 
higher fee for expedited plats as opposed to preliminary and final plats.  Discussion topics included 
an option of capping the price per lot to $2,500.00, desire to avoid deterring development and 
option to postpone for additional discussion and review.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to March 
14, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of order regarding the motion to 
postpone as it was made on top of the motion to amend.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Stefanie 

Boster advised the motion to amend could either be approved or denied prior to postponement and 
City Clerk Kris Jones advised the motion to postpone was out of order.  Mayor Collins ruled the 
motion to postpone out of order and called for a vote on the motion to amend.  Motion to amend 
failed.  Voting “no”– all members of the governing body.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to March 
14, 2022, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes”– all members of 
the governing body. 
 
 
RESOLUTION – For Governing Body approval of a one-year Bronze Boot Sponsor 
relationship between Cheyenne Frontier Days and Miller/Coors for 2022.  Mr. White moved to 
adopt, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated February 23, 
2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  City Clerk Kris Jones explained the amendment is to correct the 
sponsorship type due to incorrect information originally submitted to the Clerk’s office.  Motion 
to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6212) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – To authorize the application for the continuation grant in the amount of 
$318,375.00 from the Corporation for National and Community Service for the City of Cheyenne 
Office of Youth Alternatives Foster Grandparent Program – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Permitting the possession of open containers within the Downtown

White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Renaming Ernste Drive to Richard Martin Drive – referred to Public

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Certifying Planning Commission action amending the Cheyenne Area

Grant of Right-of-Way Easement on City Owned Property, per Exhibit A, between the City 
of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power Company for a 150’ wide right-of-way and 
easement for construction and maintenance of overhead power facilities.  Mr. White moved to 
approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated February 18, 
2022, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mayor Collins noted the substitute adds a cost to the right-of-way 
and a reversion clause so that if the right-of-way is not used, it will revert back to the city.  Motion 
to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7477) 
 

 
Contract between the City of Cheyenne and the Comea Shelter granting a subaward to be 
used to increase available emergency housing and temporary shelter to support Cheyenne’s 
homeless population (Coronavirus State and Local Fiscal Recovery Funds).  Mr. White moved to 
approve in an amount not to exceed $650,000.00, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Comment in support 
of the contract was made and it was noted the Governing Body acknowledges the concerns for the 
homeless population.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7478) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Kyle M. Brandon for 
City Public Defender legal counsel services (General Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(in an amount not to exceed $86,400.00 annually). (#7479) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Veolia ES Technical

[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Jones Land

[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Steil Surveying

[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team

Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7405 for the Contingency and Development

“Microsoft” throughout (WBC Managed Data Center Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
  
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-11-22 for furnishing eight (8) light poles (17th Street Lighting), 
for the City of Cheyenne, Engineering Department (Specific Purpose Sales Tax).  Motion (per

not to exceed $140,576.00). 
 
[CA]

No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 
Under other business:   
 
 
Mr. Laybourn acknowledged the recent passing of prior City Council member Bob Storey 
noting he was a Council member from 1981-1988 and was involved with issues relating to the 
1985 flood. 
 
 
Dr. Aldrich provided information on the upcoming Delta Kappa Gamma used book sale 
that will be held March 5-6, 2022 in the former Big Lots building at 3741 E. Lincolnway, noting 
proceeds support many local causes. 
 
 
Mr. White expressed appreciation to Mr. Johnson and Board of Public Utilities staff for 
their assistance with the ordinance amending Chapter 13.04, noting it took considerable time to

Mr. White provided information on a benefit to be held on March 2, 2022 from 5-7 p.m. at 
The Metropolitan for the South High “We The People” student civic program.   He noted students

There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-14_TotalExtraction_73921437.pdf
   
        
 
 
 
                  CITY OF CHEYENNE  
 
 
 
       PROCLAMATION 
 
 
 
WHEREAS,  
More than ninety percent (90%) of businesses throughout Wyoming 
are classified as small businesses; and 
 
WHEREAS,  
Sixty-three percent (63%) of Wyoming’s workforce is employed by 
innovative and entrepreneurial small businesses within the State; and 
  
WHEREAS,  
Business Insider has designated Wyoming as one of the best States 
in the Union for entrepreneurship; and 
 
WHEREAS,  
Collaboration with the newly formed Wyoming Innovation Partnership 
(WIP) will develop innovative solutions to support, enhance, and 
strengthen Wyoming’s economy and workforce; and 
 
WHEREAS,  
Laramie County Community College, IMPACT 307, the University of 
Wyoming, and the Wyoming Afterschool Alliance are dedicated to 
advancing education and training for future entrepreneurs across the 
State of Wyoming; and  
 
WHEREAS, 
In 2006, the United State Congress designated this week so that the 
people of the United States are reminded of the contributions of 
entrepreneurs and so that educators are encouraged to reflect on 
how entrepreneurship education can improve the performance of 
their students.  
 
 
 
NOW THEREFORE, I, Patrick J. Collins, Mayor of the City of Cheyenne, do hereby 
proclaim February 12-19, 2022, to be 
 
 
Entrepreneurship Week 
 
in the City of Cheyenne. I encourage all Cheyenne citizens to continue to be innovative and 
entrepreneurial as well as support our outstanding Cheyenne Entrepreneurs.  
 
 
IN WITNESS THEREOF, I have hereunto set my hand and caused the Seal of the City of 
Cheyenne to be affixed this 14th day of February 2022. 
 
 
 
 
 Patrick J. Collins, Mayor  


--- END OF FILE ---

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 24, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave., and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and 
Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 10, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Chapter 10.76, Abandoned Vehicles, of Title 
10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.    
Comments were made in support of the ordinance and appreciation was expressed to Captain 
David Janes, Police Department, for his efforts on the ordinance.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body. (#4412) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Chapter 8.60, Nuisances, of Title 8, Health 
and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. (#4413) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Chapter 6.08.200, Keeping of Bees, of 
Chapter 6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4414) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the Zoning Classification from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis for land located northwest of the intersection of Nationway and Logan Avenue.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry, Charles Bloom, Director of the Planning & Development 
Department, provided information on the proposed development and advised the Planning 
Commission recommended approval.  Mr. Bloom reported the development involves a demolition 

and rebuild of the current dog grooming business currently on the property.  Upon inquiry, Eric 
Fountain, Director of the Compliance Department, explained vehicles adjacent to the property 
have temporary registration and 2 shipping containers of junk have been removed from the area.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 13.04 – Public Utilities Generally; 
Chapter 13.08 – Water Service System; Chapter 13.12 – Sewer System; Chapter 13.16 – Storm 
Sewers; and Chapter 13.20 – Wastewater Treatment & Disposal; of Title 13 – Public Services, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Johnson)  
 
 
RESOLUTION –Establishing and adopting a Parking Fine Schedule for parking fees 
pursuant to City Code Section 10.60.040.  Dr. Aldrich advised the item was reviewed under other 
items for the agenda at the January 18, 2022 Finance Committee meeting, therefore, provisions of 
Title 2 of City Code have been met.  Dr. Aldrich moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. 
Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  (#6206) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing up to a $14,890,000 interfund loan from the One Percent 
Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to the Special Purpose Option Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to 
facilitate the construction of three fire stations, purchase fire trucks and replace the cemetery 
irrigation system as approved by voters on the November 2, 2021 Sixth Penny Ballot – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant under the Staffing for Adequate Fire and Emergency Response (SAFER) 
Program, on behalf of the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of adding nine 
new firefighter positions – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Certifying Planning Commission Action amending the Future Land Use 
Maps of PlanCheyenne changing the land use category from Community Business to Mixed-Use 
Commercial for property generally located west of Campbell Avenue, north of Nationway, and 
south of and adjacent to E. Lincolnway – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Cook) 
 
[CA] 
Grant of Right-of-Way Easement on City Owned Property, per Exhibit A, between the City 
of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power Company for a 150’ wide right-of-way and 
easement for construction and maintenance of overhead power facilities – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) between City of Cheyenne and Doran Lummis to 
authorize filing of platting applications(s) for City-owned lands for the purpose of access to future 
development – referred to Finance Committee. 
 

[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6957 between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming 
Cattle Co., LLC adding the Mariah Pasture and extending the term of the Belvoir Ranch Grazing 
Lease Agreement through December 31, 2024 – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #3 to Contract #6441 between the City of Cheyenne and Microsoft 
Corporation for the renewal to the City’s Enterprise Office 365 Online Enrollment – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-9-22 for furnishing three (3) new multi-purpose response vehicles 
(type 1 pumpers) and one (1) 100’ heavy duty aerial tower, for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne 
Fire & Rescue Department.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Front Range 
Fire Apparatus, Frederick, Colorado, in an amount not to exceed $3,740,216.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-7-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and S&S 
Builders, LLC, for the Street and Alley Department Tent Building Repairs and Door Repairs 
Project (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee.   
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-8-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Front 
Range Roofing Systems, LLC, for the Ice and Events Center Roof Replacement Project (General 
Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and KONE, Inc., for the repair 
of the South East Hydraulic Elevator located at the Jack Spiker Parking Garage (General Fund 
Reserves) – referred to Finance Committee. 
               
 
Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Robin Lockman advised the contract related to the Knife 
River voucher would be provided to members of the governing body.  No further comments were 
made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 
Announcement of a Public Hearing was made to be held February 14, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by 
Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for 521 Southwest Drive (located east of Southwest Drive, south 
of I-80). 
 
 
 
 
Under other business:   
 
 
Chris Layman expressed concerns relating to the 26th Street construction project which 
included: deep open holes, mud, pedestrian difficulty navigating the area, delay of project 
completion, safety hazards and major inconvenience for the last 8 months.  Ms. Layman advised 
she was given a time frame in which the project would be completed by the construction manager, 
noting that time frame has since past.  Mayor Collins noted it was anticipated the entire project 
would be completed sooner and all underground work has been done.  The remainder of the project, 
including paving the road, can’t happen until the weather improves.  Ms. Layman advised the 

project engineer assured her the hole would be back filled and graded prior to being paved, and 
she requested this be done.  Mayor Collins advised he would check with the company to see if this 
can be completed. 
 
 
 
 Jim Ridgeway provided photos of the 26th Street construction project area and voiced 
concerns with the project which included: open trenches, equipment and culverts piled up and 
fence posts being utilized incorrectly all of which are safety hazards.  Mr. Ridgeway noted 
additional citywide concerns involving construction signage which alludes to work being done in 
areas where no work is actually be done and requested signs be removed. Mayor Collins advised 
he would look into Mr. Ridgeway’s concerns.  Upon inquiry, City Engineer Tom Cobb advised 
AVI is the construction manager for the 26th Street project.   
 
 
Casey Quinn, BluePeak Fiberoptic Internet, provided information on the company noting 
they began fiberoptic internet construction locally in October.  He provided contact information 
and advised any constituent issues or concerns can be directed to him. 
 
 
Dr. Aldrich advised the City Council is challenging the Laramie County Commissioners 
during the Boys and Girls Club Chili Challenge fundraiser which will be held on Friday, January 
28, 2022, from 5-8 p.m. at Little America. 
    
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:30 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden,   
Deputy City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
January 30, 2022

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM CB 
COMMUNITY BUSINESS TO MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS FOR 
LAND LOCATED NORTHWEST OF THE INTERSECTION OF NATIONWAY 
AND LOGAN AVENUE.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, 
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on January 4, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a 
public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change. 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as the south ½ of Lot 5, 
Block 486, of the Original City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed to MUB Mixed-use Business 
Emphasis. 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases. 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

SECOND READING:

PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR

RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
Move to approve the rezoning of the South half of Lot 5, Block 486, Original City to MUB Mixed-use 
Business Emphasis as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review 
criteria for approval, with the following condition: 
1. The Future Land Use Map for the area be amended to Mixed-use Commercial prior to (or 
concurrently with) the zone change application.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS:
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-
use Business Emphasis for one-half of an original City lot. The land is developed with a single-family home, 
which was converted to business use. The purpose of the proposed zone change is to allow the demolition of 
the house and redevelopment of the site with a new commercial building.
